Brief pick (Monday): Commanders +7.5$5 at ≤50¢ → about $9.80

Game line: 49ers -7.5 (favorite) · Commanders +7.5 (-115 at kickoff) · total 51.5–52.5 · ML SF -395 to -455 / WAS +320 to +350 · Tue 9:15 a.m. JST (Mon 5:15 p.m. PT), ESPN/ABC

Won +$4.80 (+0.24u) Final: 49ers 23, Commanders 17. San Francisco won by 6, so "San Francisco wins by over 7.5" settled NO. It looked lost at halftime: 49ers 20-3, with 303 yards and 17 first downs to Washington's 90 and 2, then 23-3 with 18 seconds left in the 3rd quarter. Washington outgained San Francisco 224 to 72 after halftime and scored on two Jayden Daniels TD passes; San Francisco's last three drives were three-and-outs (7 yards total). Washington even had a chance to win: a 4th-down throw to Treylon Burks from the SF 17 was knocked loose by Malik Mustapha (1:54 left), and the last snap ended in a sack by Ogbo Okoronkwo and Romello Height. Daniels: 29 of 40, 300 yds, 2 TD, 0 INT, but only 4 runs for 10 yds. 49ers: Brock Purdy 21 of 29, 264, TD; Christian McCaffrey 16-64 and 2 short TD runs plus 9-53 receiving (one lost fumble); George Kittle 5-69 and the opening-drive TD; Mike Evans 3-94 before leaving with an Achilles injury. 4 sacks by San Francisco.

Vs. the pregame read: the forecast (49ers by 4 to 6, about 28-23) got the margin exactly (6) but the score 11 points high: 40 points, under the 51.5–52.5 total. The reason was half right. Daniels' legs, the heart of the case, did nothing (10 yards); his arm did it, against a 49ers defense without Nick Bosa or Dre Greenlaw that went soft once it led by 20 (today's Football 101). The win came from the cushion and the late "backdoor" points, not from the matchup the brief named. Kittle's TD, the risk the brief flagged, happened and didn't matter. The un-counted Commanders + Under combo also won (40 points).

Combo: USC +1.5 + Over 55.5$14.99 → $55.24

Game line: Penn State -1.5 (favorite) · USC +1.5 · total 54.5 at kickoff · Sun 8:30 a.m. JST (Sat 4:30 p.m. PT), NBC · final: USC 31, Penn State 23

Lost -$14.99 The USC leg won easily (USC won outright by 8). The Over missed by 2 points: 54 total, and the leg needed 56. Bought at about 27¢.

How it got away: 17-17 at halftime (34 points, on pace for 68). Then Penn State's offense stalled in the red zone: Ryan Barker kicked a 48-yard field goal to tie it at the half, then two second-half field goals (35 and 26) while USC scored two TDs. One Penn State TD instead of a field goal makes it 58 and your combo cashes. Football 101, Lesson 12 (why drives stall inside the 20) is the exact story. USC's Ryon Sayeri also missed a 56-yard field goal in the 2nd quarter.

Vs. the brief: the brief forecast USC by 3, about 30-27 (57 points). The side was right (USC +1.5 won for the brief too); the score came in 3 points light. The total closed at 54.5, so the market's number was almost exactly right (54).

-0.14u · this week 0-0, 1 open

  • Thursday night: the brief's lean is now a pick, Under 50.5. Wednesday's report ruled out New England's starting guards Alijah Vera-Tucker (hamstring) and Ben Brown (knee); with Mike Onwenu and Greg Van Roten already on IR, that's four guards gone. Mike Vrabel confirmed Walter Rouse and Matt Pryor start at guard, with RT Morgan Moses (foot) questionable. The catch: New England also ruled out three cornerbacks (Carlton Davis III, Karon Prunty, Channing Canada) and lists both starting safeties questionable, which helps Caleb Williams. For Chicago, RB Kyle Monangai is out and LB T.J. Edwards doubtful. Prices: Bears -3 (DraftKings -102), total 50.5 (opened 47.5 at some books, 49.5 at others); Kalshi Over 50.5 at 52¢ (Tue night). Full case and the off-switch under Best bets; today's Football 101 is about exactly this: what happens when backups play guard.
  • Tuesday's college finals (all brief passes): Middle Tennessee 26, FIU 23 as a 10-point underdog (49 points, over); Delaware 61, Missouri State 38 (Delaware -3 covered; 99 points, over 51.5–52.5 by 47); Marshall 20, South Alabama 13 as a 2.5-to-3-point underdog with QB Zion Turner (33 points, 30.5 under). Cards under Latest finals.
  • Monday's injuries, now known: Commanders WR Terry McLaurin tore his patellar tendon and is out for the season. 49ers WR Mike Evans has Achilles tendinitis, "not a tear" (NFL Network's Mike Garafolo), and needs no further testing; Kyle Shanahan: "you don't think it's a long-term thing." San Francisco is "hopeful" for Sunday at Atlanta, but NBC Sports Bay Area reported he could sit until after the Week 8 bye.
  • Wednesday practice (NFL.com): Lamar Jackson (ankle) was limited, his first work since the injury (Bengals at Ravens, BAL -2.5); Baker Mayfield (thumb) practiced fully, decision Friday (Bucs at Panthers); the Giants start J.J. McCarthy at Houston; the Rams signed K Brandon McManus (Blake Grupe tore his ACL) and put DE Kobie Turner on IR; Saquon Barkley is dealing with a hamstring injury; Jalen Hurts is reportedly out for the season.
  • Tonight's college game, a pass: New Mexico State -2.5 at Sam Houston, total 44.5 (opened 46.5); Dimers has NMSU 59.8%, about 25-21, right on the number.
  • College news: Penn State is on its bye (Purdue at home Oct 31, time not yet announced); Hawai'i (1-6) visits winless Northern Illinois Saturday, Hawai'i -6.5. Saturday's top games: Ole Miss at Texas, Indiana at Michigan, Houston at Utah, Texas A&M at Alabama (lines under This week).

Pregame analysis below, kept for the record.

Mon Oct 12 · 5:15 p.m. PT · ESPN/ABC · SoFi Stadium (roof; weather doesn't matter) · lines from VegasInsider and TheSpread (Mon a.m.); Kalshi prices via DeFi Rate (Mon ~1:45 a.m. PT)

MarketVegas (open → now)Kalshi
SpreadRams -2.5/-3 → Rams -3.5 (favorite) · Bills +3.5"Rams win by over 3.5": 48¢ · by over 2.5: 57¢
WinnerRams -155 → -180 · Bills +150Rams 62¢ · Bills 39¢
Total52.5–53.5 → 54.5Over 54.5: 50¢ · Over 52.5: 56¢
Kyren Williams rush yds69.5–7070+: about 47¢

The gap between "Rams by over 2.5" (57¢) and "by over 3.5" (48¢) is Kalshi's price on the Rams winning by exactly 3: about 9%. That's the key number 3 (Market 101, Lesson 2).

The matchup in numbers

  • Records: Bills 3-1 (beat Houston 36-31, Detroit, the Chargers 24-16; lost 29-26 to New England as a 6.5-point favorite). Rams 2-2 (lost 27-7 to the 49ers in Melbourne and 30-26 at Denver; beat the Giants 28-6 and won 24-20 at Philadelphia).
  • Strength vs. strength: Buffalo's offense is 2nd in EPA per play (+0.20); the Rams' defense is 3rd (-0.13) and allows a 38.8% success rate. The other matchup is the mismatch: the Rams' offense is about 11th (+0.03) against a Bills defense that's 28th–30th (allows a 48.1% success rate and 274 passing yards a game). FTN's DVOA after Week 4: Bills 4th, Rams 7th; Rams special teams 32nd.
  • Injuries: Bills DT Ed Oliver (their best interior lineman) and WR Joshua Palmer are out; WR DJ Moore (shoulder) is questionable after a limited Saturday practice. Josh Allen isn't on the report and denied a knee rumor Thursday. Rams: S Quentin Lake (hip) questionable, "trending in the right direction"; Puka Nacua is healthy (90.5% of snaps in Week 4 after missing Weeks 2–3).
  • Where the money is: at Circa, the Rams have 38% of spread tickets but 88% of the money (VSiN); CLEATZ shows 74% of the handle on the Rams. The public is on the Bills. The Over has 79% of bets and 86% of money. Splits are a tiebreaker, not a reason (Lesson 16).
  • The case for the Under: CBS SportsLine's model projects about 50 points. Buffalo plays zone 77.9% of the time, and Stafford is 31st in catchable-pass rate against zone (NBC's Dalzell). Rams home games are 2-0 to the Under.

The brief's read

  • Spread: pass. The market's Rams -3.5 matches the numbers (ESPN FPI had the Rams about 60% before the weekend; Kalshi says 62%). The sharp money on the Rams and the key number 3 on the Bills' side point opposite ways, and the checklist has no specific, checkable reason the price is wrong. Expert picks are split too (24 national experts: Rams 14, Bills 10).
  • Total: pass. Big money on the Over vs. a model and a zone-coverage matchup that point Under. 54.5 sits between the key numbers 51 and 55 (Lesson 17), so neither side gets a cushion.
  • Forecast: Rams by 3 to 4, about 29-25. The Rams' best path is the Bills-defense mismatch: running the ball and keeping Allen on the sideline, which is why the brief's one bet is on the Rams' running back.

Pregame check, 4:10 p.m. PT Kyren pick stands

  • Inactives are out. Bills: WR DJ Moore (shoulder), WR Joshua Palmer, DL Ed Oliver, DL Landon Jackson, CB Dee Alford, OL Jude Bowry, G Ar'maj Reed-Adams. Rams: WR CJ Daniels, QB Ty Simpson, TE Max Klare, DE Larrell Murchison, OL Bill Murray. Kyren Williams and Blake Corum are not on the list, so both are active. S Quentin Lake is active.
  • What Moore's absence means: Buffalo loses its top receiver, so Keon Coleman, Khalil Shakir and Dalton Kincaid get more targets. A weaker Bills passing game makes it more likely the Rams lead and run late, which helps the Kyren pick. It nudges the total down a little, but the brief still passes on it.
  • Closing lines: Rams -3.5 (Bet365 -102), moneyline Rams -163 / 62¢ on Kalshi, total 54.5 (Over 52¢ on Polymarket). DraftKings moved Kyren's line to Over 69.5 at -114 (about 53%), so the market has drifted toward the over since this morning.
  • Workload check: over the last two weeks Kyren has out-carried Corum 31-11 (74% of the backs' carries) at 5.4 yards a carry; Buffalo is 29th in EPA allowed per run.
  • The call: Kyren Williams: 70+ rushing yards → YES, $5, at 50¢ or less. If Kalshi now shows 51–52¢, it's still a small edge; above 52¢, pass. Spread and total: still pass.

On Kalshi: Kyren Williams: 70+ rushing yards → YES, at 50¢ or less (about 47¢ this morning). Wins at 70 yards or more. Why: his 2026 game log is 41, 85, 88, 80 (3 of 4 over 70; the one miss was the Week 1 loss); Buffalo's defense is 28th–30th by EPA and success rate and is missing Ed Oliver in the middle; the Rams are favored, and a team ahead runs late. Brief's estimate: about 55% vs. a 47¢ price. Kalshi combo (not counted; lost both legs)

Kyren 70+ and the Rams to win. In one combo, tap "Yes" on Kyren Williams: 70+ rushing yards and "Yes" on Los Angeles R wins. These legs move together (if the Rams win, they probably ran well), so the fair price is a bit above the simple 55% × 62% ≈ 34¢: about 36¢ (Lesson 13). Buy only at 33¢ or less, $5 max. Not counted as a pick.

Player props: looked at and passed

  • Josh Allen: 1+ touchdowns → YES at 53¢. He has a rushing TD in all four games (7 total). Tempting, but sportsbooks price it the same (about 52%), so there's no gap to take.
  • James Cook 75+ rushing yards (book line 74.5): 57, 135, 154, 75. Two monster games inflate his average; the Rams' run defense is 14th. Coin flip.
  • Puka Nacua 93+ receiving yards (book line 92.5–95.5): only two games logged (74, 125). Too little history for a line that high.
  • Stafford over 278.5 passing yards: 155, 327, 390, 317, but the zone-coverage stat above cuts against it. Pass.

49ers at Seahawks: why it's the best bet

  • The model and the market disagree by about 4.5 points. ESPN's FPI has San Francisco by 1.4 (56% to win); the market has Seattle by 3 to 3.5. A gap that size between a respected power rating and the line is the first thing the checklist looks for.
  • The football reason: San Francisco (4-0) is 1st in the NFL in offensive EPA per play and 1st in yards per play (6.8), scoring 30.5 a game (Sharp Football Analysis). Seattle's defense is elite too (2nd in defensive EPA, 1st in yards per play allowed at 4.0), so this is strength on strength, which usually means a close game. Close games favor the team getting 3.5.
  • The key number: +3.5 wins on a 3-point Seattle win, the most common NFL margin (Market 101, Lesson 2). That's why the brief wants 3.5, not 3.
  • The risks: Nick Bosa is out, and the 49ers' pressure rate in the two games without him is 28.9%; Sam Darnold will have time. Seattle has won nine straight at home and is 1st in DVOA (Covers). Mike Evans plays through cracked ribs; FB Kyle Juszczyk is out. Seattle is without RB Zach Charbonnet.
  • The money is split (one tracker says 85% of money on Seattle, another 86% on San Francisco), so it isn't a tiebreaker either way. Weather is fine: 58°F, dry, wind 9–10 mph.

Final: Ravens 30, Falcons 23 (OT) Bijan 70+ won

  • How it went: Atlanta led 14-9 at half (Jessie Bates fumble-return TD, Kyle Pitts TD from Penix) and 23-15 after Nick Folk field goals of 23, 34 and 48. Tyler Huntley threw a 7-yard TD to Devontez Walker with 0:44 left and Derrick Henry ran in the 2-point try: 23-23. Baltimore scored a TD in overtime and Atlanta couldn't answer.
  • Penix's ribs: he was driven into the ground by a pass rusher late in the 4th, left under his own power, returned briefly, and was done by overtime: Tua Tagovailoa's interception by Kyle Hamilton ended the game. MRI today; Penix says "I'm going to be ready to go next week." Next: Bears at Falcons, Sun Oct 18, 10 a.m. PT, FOX.
  • Vs. the pregame read: right on Bijan 70+ (the brief's prop, +0.12u; final 17 carries, 86 yards). Derrick Henry ran for 102 plus an 18-yard TD catch; Huntley 24 of 32, 201 yards, 2 TD and the OT-winning sneak. Wrong on the script: Huntley wasn't the liability the brief expected (2 TD passes, no INTs); Baltimore ran for nearly 200 yards against the league's best run defense on paper, and controlled the clock. The brief passed on the spread (Falcons -3/-3.5); the Ravens won outright.

Sources: stats via SportRadar · Heavy (Penix) · ClutchPoints (Tua)

Ravens at Falcons (SNF): the Lamar effect

  • The line moved about 7 points (Ravens -3.5 → Falcons -3 to -3.5) when Lamar Jackson (ankle) was ruled out; Footballguys expects 2–3 weeks. That move already prices in most of the drop to Tyler Huntley.
  • Huntley: 5-7 in 12 career starts; completes 44.4% of passes under pressure, and Atlanta leads the NFL in pressure rate (46.3%). In relief last week he went 8 of 9 for 63 yards and Baltimore scored on none of his five drives. Ravens C Cam Jurgens and edge Trey Hendrickson are out; Zay Flowers and Marlon Humphrey are questionable.
  • Atlanta's run game: since Michael Penix Jr. returned, Atlanta leads the NFL in rushing EPA per play (+0.28) and rushing success rate (58.2%), and has averaged 40 points a game. Baltimore has allowed six rushing TDs, tied for the most.
  • Bijan Robinson's game log (rushing yards): 83, 76, 194, 145. He's cleared 70 in all four games, including the two with Penix out. Sportsbooks have him at 86.5–91.5 yards. With Huntley at QB, the likely script is Atlanta ahead and running the ball late, which is more carries for Bijan.
  • Derrick Henry (line 80.5, down from 89.5 Wednesday): 144, 68, 89, 73. He's gone under his line three straight weeks, and Atlanta allows 2.7 yards a carry (48.3 rushing yards a game, 1st). But without Jackson he averages 24 carries over his career, so volume can carry him to 80. Too close: pass.

Giants at Commanders: weather and a 3.5-point line

  • Rain and wind from Isaias' remnants: sustained 13 mph, gusts to 28. Market 101, Lesson 12: wind over 15 mph is what lowers passing and kicking. The total already fell from 44.5 to 41.5.
  • Why points are worth more in bad weather: fewer possessions and fewer points mean a 3.5-point head start covers more of the possible outcomes.
  • The teams: the Giants are 3-1 (beat Arizona 36-24 last week); Washington is 1-3. Jayden Daniels returns in an elbow brace; Stefon Diggs is out and CB Trey Amos was downgraded to out Saturday. 81% of DraftKings' spread money is on the Giants.

UCLA at Oregon: why it's the best bet

  • Oregon's QB is out. Dante Moore is in concussion protocol. Dylan Raiola (the Nebraska transfer) makes his first Oregon start; he has 413 yards, 5 TDs and 0 INTs in two relief appearances. Good numbers, but a first start in a top-25 game is a different job.
  • The mismatch: UCLA's offense is 1st nationally in EPA per rush; RB Wayne Wright has 445 yards and 9 TDs on 49 carries (9.1 a carry). Oregon's defense is 101st against the run by the same measure. That's the same pattern that won Thursday (Tampa 241 rushing yards) and lost the brief Friday's under (Iowa 263): the team that can run on a weak front controls the game.
  • Overall strength is close: GameOnPaper's net adjusted EPA per play has UCLA 14th and Oregon 17th. A 10.5-point spread says Oregon is much better; the play-by-play data says they're about even, with home field worth about 3.
  • The money agrees: UCLA +11.5 has only 30% of bets but 88% of the money (CLEATZ). Per the checklist, that's the tiebreaker, not the reason. And the line has already come down a point at some books (-11.5 → -10.5), so grab the bigger number if you see it.
  • Risk: Oregon at home rarely gets run over, and the 97% of bets on the over says the public expects points. If Raiola starts hot, Oregon can pull away.

Georgia at Alabama: hurricane weather and a coin-flip line

  • Weather: Hurricane Isaias was forecast to make landfall near Pensacola Friday night and could reach central Alabama Saturday afternoon. Kickoff is unchanged (SEC and Alabama sources via CBS and Athlon). Tuesday's Tuscaloosa forecast: 90% rain, about 1.2 inches, wind around 20 mph gusting to 38. Market 101, Lesson 12: wind over 15 mph is what actually lowers passing and kicking; rain alone matters less.
  • The QBs: Alabama's Keelon Russell, a first-year starter: 1,414 yards, 11 TDs, 2 INTs, 184 rushing yards, with 13 TDs and no turnovers in his last three games. Georgia's Gunner Stockton: about 75% completions, 13 TDs, 1 INT.
  • The defenses: Georgia allows 57.4 rushing yards a game (3rd nationally). Alabama's defense is 9th in rush success rate allowed and leads the SEC with 13 takeaways.
  • Injuries: Georgia is without RB Chauncey Bowens, WR Thomas Blackshear, G Marcus Harrison, S Tyriq Green and OLB Amaris Williams; RB Nate Frazier (ribs) is expected to play. Alabama's report wasn't available to the brief this morning; the 2:30 p.m. check covers it.
  • The money: Alabama's moneyline has 37% of bets but 91% of the money, while 71% of the spread money is on Georgia +1.5. Big bettors split, which is why the line sits at a near pick'em.
  • History: Kirby Smart is 0-7 against Alabama in the regular season. That's a headline, not a reason: the brief doesn't price streaks.

Vs. the pregame read

  • Right: Tampa's defense kept Dallas to 10 points (Dallas averaged 58 combined points a game in its over streak). Dallas has completed only 9 of 20 passes, and Tampa forced field-goal range into a field goal before half, exactly the "drives stall, field goals keep it close" case for the pick.
  • Better than expected: the rookie. Daniels is 12 of 16 with a TD and no turnovers, mostly short throws (only 18 air yards), and Tampa has held the ball for almost two-thirds of the half. Short, safe drives shorten the game, which helps a team getting points.
  • Watch: Tampa has 8 penalties; Dallas's passing (48-yard long) can flip this quickly if Prescott starts hitting.

Second-half forecast

Dallas is still the better team and should outscore Tampa in the second half by about 4 points on average, which puts the most likely final around Dallas by 7 (the most common NFL margin). Dallas needs to outscore Tampa by 7+ in the second half to beat your +9.5; the brief puts that at roughly 40%, so your bet is about a 60% favorite now. Pregame analysis (kept for the record)

5:15 p.m. PT · ESPN · Caesars Superdome (indoors) · both teams 1-2

Vegas vs. Kalshi

Who winsSaints -130 / Falcons +110Saints 54¢ / Falcons 46¢Same read: Saints about 54%. Falcons pay a bit more on Kalshi ($10 → $21.74 vs. $21.00).

Total47.5 to 48.5Over 47.5: 50¢A true coin flip

The spread sits just under 3, the most common NFL margin of victory. A Saints win by exactly 3 beats Falcons +2.5, so getting +3 anywhere is worth a few cents.

The matchup

  • Atlanta's strength hits New Orleans' weakness. The Falcons are 2nd in the NFL in rushing (173 yards a game); Bijan Robinson has 349 yards in three games (83, 72 and 194 with 2 TDs against Green Bay). The Saints are 27th against the run.
  • And New Orleans is short-handed exactly there. Out: LB Kaden Elliss and edge rushers Carl Granderson and Anfernee Jennings. LB Pete Werner is questionable. That's the injury "cluster" Market 101, Lesson 11 warned about: three missing players in the front seven, the group that stops the run.
  • The Saints' case: they're 3rd in passing (Tyler Shough to Chris Olave), and Atlanta lost its top cornerback, A.J. Terrell, to injured reserve. They also lost RB Travis Etienne to IR, so Alvin Kamara carries the run game.
  • Atlanta's defense is 1st against the run (47.7 yards allowed a game), so New Orleans will be one-dimensional. The Saints are also 27th in turnover margin.

Risks: Penn State has 13 players out, including cornerbacks Joshua Johnson, Tyler Armstead and Christian Askew and defensive ends LaVar Arrington II, Max Granville, Dayshaun Burnett and Mason Robinson; it has lost three straight Big Ten games and has 116 rushing yards in its last two. A Michigan win by exactly 7 loses the bet. Off if Sowell or Cam Smith is ruled out on Friday's report (Sat 9:00 a.m. JST / Fri 5:00 p.m. PT) or Saturday's (Sun 2:30 a.m. JST / Sat 10:30 a.m. PT), or if the price is above 51¢. The brief checks both reports. Forecast: Michigan by 3 to 4, about 22-19.

Penn State controlled the ball (14:29 to 9:17 at 6:14 of the 2nd), Michigan had 26 rushing yards and threw an interception, which is the pregame case playing out. If you didn't buy before kickoff, live "No" prices will be much higher now; don't chase (Lesson 8). Settles in Monday's edition.

Broncos +3.5 vs. Seahawks¼u · $5

What to tap: the Kalshi market "Seattle wins by over 3.5 points" → NO, at 57¢ or less. It wins if Denver wins, or Seattle wins by 1, 2 or 3. At a sportsbook the same idea is Broncos +3 (-120).

Why (the checklist): (1) Market price: Seattle -2.5 to -3, Kalshi Seattle 59¢ to win; OddsShopper's simulations have Seattle 57%. From those numbers, Denver +3.5 should win about 57% of the time, so 57¢ is the market's fair price. (2) The specific reason: Seattle's offense is 26th in EPA per play and 30th running the ball, and Zach Charbonnet is out, so Seattle can't lean on the run against Denver's thin secondary. Sam Darnold has 5 INTs in three weeks and was sacked 8 times in two games; Denver's defense sacked Justin Herbert 5 times Sunday. (3) History: Denver is 22-8 straight up at home under Sean Payton; Broncos games have gone under 4 of 5. (4) Cushion: with a 40.5 total, a field goal is a bigger share of the game, so 3.5 points (it covers a Seattle win by exactly 3) is worth more than usual. (5) Splits (tiebreaker only): Circa's money is "emphatically" on Denver even though most tickets are on Seattle (VSiN). Brief's estimate: about 60% vs. a 57¢ price, a small edge, hence ¼ unit.

Risks: Denver is likely without CB Pat Surtain II and S Talanoa Hufanga, and CB Riley Moss (ribs) is limited. That's Jaxon Smith-Njigba (603 yards, 7 TDs) against backups. Denver's own offense is 24th in EPA. Off if Moss is also ruled out and Smith-Njigba practices fully (three starting defensive backs gone is too many), or if the price climbs above 57¢.

Final check (Thu 10:15 p.m. JST / 6:15 a.m. PT): go. Surtain and Hufanga are officially out; Moss is questionable and expected to play; Smith-Njigba practiced fully. Seattle also loses two linebackers (Drake Thomas, Chazz Surratt) and its No. 2 back. The market moved slightly toward Denver: books went from -3 to -2.5 everywhere, and Kalshi's "Seattle by over 3.5" Yes price slid from 44¢ (Tue) to 42¢ (Wed), so "No" now costs about 58–59¢. That's 1–2¢ over the limit: set a 57¢ order and only take it there. The only remaining trigger is Moss on the inactives list (Fri 7:45 a.m. JST / Thu 3:45 p.m. PT). Forecast: Seattle by 1 to 3, about 20-18.

Won +$3.77 (+0.19u) Final: Broncos 17, Seahawks 14 (OT). Denver won outright. 5 sacks, 2 INTs, a fumble; Darnold 27 of 51. Smith-Njigba got his (12-134), as the risk note warned, and it didn't matter.

Lost -$5 (-0.25u) Final: 9 carries, 61 yards (Bears 31-23). He had 51 on 4 carries in the first half, then Chicago's lead (21-7 at the half, 28-7 after three) took the run away: Atlanta threw 33 times. What the brief learns: once Caleb Williams was confirmed, the game-script item (checklist #10) had turned from a plus to a minus, and the brief kept the bet because the book line went up. A rising prop line says the market expects more yards on average; it doesn't protect against the one script (falling behind early) that kills a running-back over. Next time a confirmed starter flips the script, the pick gets cut to a pass.

How the brief picks bets

"Pass" means no bet. If the brief can't point to a specific, checkable reason that the price is wrong, it recommends nothing on that game, even if it has an opinion on who wins. Most games end up as passes; that's on purpose.

The checklist (every pick, every time)

  1. Start with the market price. No-vig sportsbook line (Market 101, Lesson 1) and the Kalshi price. Assume they're right until proven otherwise.
  2. Find a specific reason the price is off, backed by efficiency data, not just yards or records: a strength-vs-weakness mismatch (e.g., Atlanta's outside-zone runs vs. the league's worst defense against them), an injury cluster, rest or travel, a QB change.
  3. Check the history behind the headline number. Game-by-game logs, not just averages (Bijan's "116" is really 83, 72, 194). Check how the stat changes when a team is ahead or behind.
  4. Read the sharper sources (list below), and note when everyone agrees: if every preview likes the same side, the price has usually moved already.
  5. Compare Vegas and Kalshi. A gap of 3¢+ means the cheaper side gets a closer look (Lesson 7).
  6. Pick the line with cushion: +2.5 or +3 over +1.5 (3 is the most common margin); prop lines below a player's typical game, not his average.
  7. Set a top price = the brief's estimate minus a small margin. Above it, skip.
  8. Size by confidence: ½ unit for a clear reason, ¼ unit for props and combos. Make sure your bets don't work against each other.
  9. Check inactives (90 minutes before kickoff) and weather. The brief schedules this; it isn't your job.
  10. Game script for player props. A running back's over needs a weak run defense and a team likely to lead (Kyren lost when the Rams trailed); a quarterback's rushing or a receiver's yards need the opposite script. Ask "who is ahead in the 4th quarter?" before any prop. Added Oct 19, after Bijan 70+ lost on 9 carries: if late quarterback news makes the other team better, a running-back over becomes a pass, even if the prop line moves up.
  11. Track the closing price. If the price moves our way before kickoff, the pick found value even if it loses (Lesson 6). This is the real test of the brief over time.

Sources the brief now uses (beyond the standard previews)

TypeSourcesUsed for
Efficiency (NFL)EPA per play and success rate (Sharp Football Analysis, rbsdm.com, nfelo), Next Gen Stats (NFL.com), PFF grades, FTN (DVOA)Which team is actually better play for play, by run and pass, on offense and defense
Efficiency (college)SP+ (ESPN, Bill Connelly), FEI, CollegeFootballData.comCollege ratings that adjust for schedule strength
History and game logsPro Football Reference, Sports Reference (college), ESPN/CBS game logsPlayer and team history, splits (home/road, win/loss), typical game vs. average
MarketConsensus lines (Covers, VegasInsider), Kalshi, betting splitsThe starting price, line movement, where big money is
InjuriesTeam reports, inactives, beat writersClusters and late scratches

Limits: some of these (full PFF grades, FTN's DVOA) are partly behind paywalls; the brief uses what's published publicly from them. The brief has no betting model of its own, so its probabilities are informed estimates and are labeled that way.

Last Sunday (Oct 4) finals: your games vs. the brief's forecasts

Kept from Sunday's check-ins, with Sunday night added.

Patriots 29, Bills 26 (your combo leg: lost)

  • Pregame: Buffalo -6.5, ~74%. 12:20 forecast: Patriots 24-20, Bills ~38%. The winner was right; it was a lot wilder.
  • 4th quarter: a Bills field goal made it 21-20. Then Josh Allen hit Keon Coleman for a 34-yard TD with 4:33 left: Bills 26-21, but the 2-point try failed. Drake Maye answered with a 23-yard TD to Efton Chism with 1:55 left, plus a 2-point catch: Patriots 29-26.
  • Why New England won: it had the ball 34 minutes to 26, outgained Buffalo 405-340, and Maye threw 3 TDs. The brief flagged New England's running game early; it kept Buffalo's offense off the field all day.

Chiefs 30, Raiders 27 (your Raiders +5.5 and the brief's +4.5: won)

  • Pregame: KC -4.5. Brief said the Raiders' pass rush and home field would keep it close.
  • How it went: Raiders led 13-10 at half and 19-17 with 5 minutes left. Kansas City then scored two TDs in 15 seconds of game clock (both by Kenneth Walker, the second a 22-yard run): 30-19. A Kirk Cousins TD to Brock Bowers plus a 2-point catch made it 30-27 with 1:46 left, so both +4.5 and +5.5 covered. The onside kick failed.
  • The real story: Las Vegas outgained KC 437-399 and Cousins threw for 365, but four drives ended in field goals instead of TDs. The pass rush the brief liked got 2 sacks; the Chiefs ran for 7.3 yards a carry.

49ers 24, Broncos 14 (Broncos +2.5/+3 and Over 48.5: lost)

  • Pregame: SF -3, total 46.5. Brief's reason for Denver: San Francisco, missing three pass rushers, would have to blitz and give up big plays.
  • What happened: the 49ers got zero sacks, but Denver never hit a big play either. Bo Nix threw 42 times for just 214 yards; Denver's passes traveled only 34 yards in the air total, almost all short throws. The game was 3-3 at half, which sank the over early.
  • The finish: SF led 17-6; Nix found Jaylen Waddle for a TD plus 2 points to make it 17-14 with 4:10 left (Broncos +3 alive). Then Brock Purdy hit George Kittle for 56 yards. 49ers 24-14.
  • Brief's miss: the read on the 49ers' pass rush was right, but it assumed Denver would take deep shots. It didn't. Lesson for the brief: a weak pass rush only matters if the other offense throws deep.

Seahawks 30, Chargers 23 (combo leg ✓; Smith-Njigba bets lost)

  • Pregame: SEA -7.5. Brief expected Seattle to lead and keep throwing to Smith-Njigba.
  • What happened: Seattle led 30-13 but ran instead (138 rushing yards), so Smith-Njigba had 5 catches for 76 yards, 4 short of the brief's 80 and 24 short of your 100. Seattle's two TD catches went to Eric Wilson and Tory Horton. The Chargers turned it over 4 times; 30 penalties combined slowed everything down. Uchenna Nwosu's strip-sack with 54 seconds left sealed it.

Other combo legs

  • Ravens 24, Titans 18 ✓ (12:20 forecast 31-13). Baltimore didn't score after halftime; Tennessee's 8 fourth-quarter points made it closer, but the leg won.
  • Vikings 15, Dolphins 10 ✓ A grind, as the 38.5 total suggested. Minnesota won but didn't cover 9.5.
  • Colts 30, Commanders 13 ✓ (morning game).

Sunday night: Panthers 32, Lions 26

  • Pregame: DET -3.5, total 50.5, Lions ~65%. Result: Carolina won outright, and 58 points went over.
  • How: Bryce Young was 29 of 41 for 329 yards and 2 TDs, both to rookie Tetairoa McMillan (32 and 10 yards). Chuba Hubbard ran for 2 TDs. Carolina gained 445 yards, held the ball for 33 minutes, and had zero turnovers.
  • Detroit's problem: Jared Goff threw for 412 yards, but the Lions ran only 15 times (46 yards) and kept stalling near the goal line: 4 field goals and just 2 TDs on 11 red-zone pass attempts. Field goals instead of touchdowns is how a team outgains its opponent and still loses (same story as the Raiders on Sunday).
  • The brief's conditional over: Carolina played without CBs Jaycee Horn and Mike Jackson, the exact trigger for buying Over 50.5. The check wasn't done, so no bet was signaled. It would have won.

Penn State desk

3-4 (0-4 Big Ten) · lost 21-18 at Michigan · covered +6.5 (brief's pick won) · bye this week · next: Purdue at home, Sat Oct 31 (time and TV not announced)

This week (bye)

  • No QB change: Matt Campbell said switching from Rocco Becht has "never been a consideration": Becht "has to be better," and Campbell has "complete faith" in him. Campbell called Becht's second interception "a bad interception" (he hesitated between scrambling and throwing). The backups are Alex Manske (Iowa State transfer, 3 games played) and Connor Barry (Division III transfer, third string).
  • The bye's job: "detailed things that are costing us." Michigan scored all 21 points off Penn State's 3 turnovers. The staff is also asking the Big Ten about a possible safety and an offensive pass interference call.
  • Health: WR Koby Howard (344 receiving yards through 3 games, team high) and DE LaVar Arrington II (hasn't played this season) are to be re-evaluated after the bye; LB Alex Tatsch is out for the season; LB Cam Smith is practicing.
  • Recruiting hit: 2027 QB Will Wood, Penn State's only quarterback commit (No. 30 QB in the 247 Composite), flipped to Boston College on Sunday.

What happened at Michigan

  • A 3-0 halftime lead, then three turnovers: Penn State controlled the first half (Michigan 80 yards on 23 plays) but only scored a Ryan Barker 24-yd field goal off a 16-play drive. In the 3rd quarter Rocco Becht threw two interceptions (Rod Moore Jr., Chris Bracy) that became Michigan TDs: Bryson Kuzdzal's 43-yd run and Jordan Marshall's 1-yd run at the end of a 74-yd drive. Per a reporter at Campbell's press conference, all three Michigan TDs followed Penn State turnovers (the two INTs and a sack-fumble).
  • Michigan changed quarterbacks: Bryce Underwood (5 of 11, 43 yds, 2 INTs) was benched at halftime for freshman Tommy Carr, Lloyd Carr's grandson (5 of 7, 47, the TD run). Michigan ran for 211 yards (Savion Hiter 11-78); Penn State ran for 65 (Carson Hansen 15-51, TD).
  • The comeback that fell short: Hansen's 2-yd TD and a 2-point pass to Ben Brahmer, then Becht to Chase Sowell for 24 yards with 1:11 left (73 yds, 11 plays). Sowell finished with 11 catches for 151 yards, a career high, despite the early ankle scare. Michigan recovered the onside kick at its 49 and ran out the clock. Becht: 28 of 44, 267, TD, 2 INT.
  • Defense: LB Tony Rojas had a career-high 12 tackles and a sack; Josiah Zayas intercepted Underwood.
  • Matt Campbell: "We didn't stop the run, and we certainly didn't take care of the football." On Becht: "I have complete faith in who Rocco Becht is." He said "the margin for error right now is razor thin" and that Penn State will ask the Big Ten to review a call before halftime and a possible safety blown dead by a quick whistle.
  • Betting: the brief's Penn State +6.5 won (+$4.80 on $5); 39 points went under 42.5.

Next: bye week, then Purdue at home · Sat Oct 31 (time and TV not yet announced)

  • The bye should get WR Koby Howard (out "until after the bye") and DE LaVar Arrington II (out through the bye) back for Purdue. Purdue is 3-4 after losing 38-26 to Washington on Friday; its games are 6 of 7 over the total.
  • The brief will post the Purdue line when it opens (Sunday night, Oct 25 US time). Thursday check: no new injury or depth-chart news found since Tuesday.
Michigan week notes (before and during the game)

At Michigan · Sun Oct 18, 4:30 a.m. JST (Sat Oct 17, 12:30 p.m. PT) · CBS/Paramount+

  • Halftime: Penn State 3, Michigan 0 (latest verified). Rocco Becht led a 16-play opening drive that stalled in the red zone (Lesson 12 again) for Ryan Barker's field goal. CB Zion Tracy intercepted Bryce Underwood in the 2nd quarter. At 6:14 of the 2nd: Becht 11 of 14, 87 yds; Chase Sowell 5-60; Penn State 111 total yards to 60, but only 24 rushing yards (1.8 a carry). Sowell rolled his right ankle on the second drive and returned taped (a 26-yard contested catch after). DT Armstrong Nnodim was a game-time decision; Cael Brezina played with a club-style brace on his right hand. Brief's PSU +6.5: winning. Final in Monday's edition.
  • Tuesday's press conferences: Matt Campbell talked about the run game, the timeout mistakes against USC and "not wavering" from the program's standards. OC Taylor Mouser called improving the run game "our biggest focus" (inconsistent "from the calls to the people to the schemes") and said one catch for TE Ben Brahmer against USC was "unacceptable." Campbell likes the Carson Hansen-James Peoples rotation more than Hansen alone.
  • Availability: WR Koby Howard is out until after the bye (Purdue on Oct 31 is a possible return); DE LaVar Arrington II is out through the bye; LB Alex Tatsch's timeline is unknown; LB Cam Smith could return this week, which matters against a Michigan team that wants to run. No public update on WR Chase Sowell's leg yet; the first availability report is Thu 9:00 a.m. JST (Wed 5:00 p.m. PT).
  • Line: Michigan -6 → -6.5 (DraftKings Penn State +6.5 at -102, moneyline +200), total 42.5. CBS SportsLine's model has Penn State covering in "well over 60%" of simulations: Michigan has covered only 2 of its last 8 games after a bye, has the Big Ten's second-worst scoring offense and second-most turnovers, and the model projects Rocco Becht for 200+ passing yards with Penn State running for 130+.
  • Michigan is 3-2 (0-2 Big Ten) under first-year coach Kyle Whittingham with QB Bryce Underwood, coming off a bye. Wins: Western Michigan 13-12 (last-second Hail Mary), Oklahoma 17-10, UTEP 52-17. Losses: Iowa 20-19 (walk-off TD) and 20-13 at Minnesota. A low-scoring team, hence the low total.
  • Penn State injuries: WR Chase Sowell hurt his right leg just before halftime against USC (4 catches, 95 yards at the break), came back for one drive and looked limited; no diagnosis yet. WR Koby Howard is out about three games. The first availability report is Thu 9:00 a.m. JST (Wed 5:00 p.m. PT).
  • Matt Campbell: "We're not where we need to be yet," and "We will find a pathway forward." Penn State has 116 rushing yards in its last two Big Ten games.
  • Thursday's availability report (the one the pick waited for): Penn State lists WR Chase Sowell, LB Cam Smith, DT Armstrong Nnodim and DE Yvan Kemajou as probable. Out: WR Koby Howard, DEs Max Granville, LaVar Arrington II, Dayshaun Burnett and Mason Robinson, TE Cooper Alexander, CBs Joshua Johnson, Tyler Armstead and Christian Askew, LB Alex Tatsch, OL Will Tompkins, DT Liam Andrews, K Cristiano Rosa. Michigan: OL Andrew Sprague and Ace Hamilton out, OT Andrew Babalola, OL Brady Norton and Jake Guarnera questionable, WR Kendrick Bell doubtful, WR Braydon Alford, EDGE Julius Holly and RB Micah Ka'apana out. Next reports: Sat 9:00 a.m. JST (Fri 5:00 p.m. PT) and Sun 2:30 a.m. JST (Sat 10:30 a.m. PT); the brief reads both.
  • Brief's call (Fri): Penn State +6.5 is the best bet. On Kalshi, "Michigan wins by over 6.5" → NO, ¼u ($5), at 51¢ or less. Dimers still has Michigan 73%, about 26-18; the brief's estimate is Michigan by 3 to 4 (about 22-19). Full reasoning under Best bets.
  • Friday's availability report (Sat 9:00 a.m. JST / Fri 5:00 p.m. PT): no change for Penn State: Sowell, Cam Smith, DT Armstrong Nnodim and DE Yvan Kemajou probable; the same 13 players out. Michigan: OT Andrew Sprague out; OT Andrew Babalola, C Jake Guarnera, G Brady Norton, DEs John Henry Daley and Dom Nichols questionable; punt returner Andrew Marsh probable. Final reports Sun 2:30 a.m. JST (Sat 10:30 a.m. PT).
  • Saturday prices: Michigan -6.5 (DraftKings -115), total 42.5, ML Michigan -250 / Penn State +210. Kalshi: Michigan 67¢ to win; "Michigan wins by over 6.5" Yes 49¢ / No 52¢; Over 42.5 52¢. Covers likes Penn State +6.5 and the Under; DraftKings Network's writer takes Michigan -6.5 (27-16). Penn State ranks 18th in adjusted defensive EPA per play (Covers). Brief: still Penn State +6.5 at 51¢ or less.

Hawai'i desk

1-6 (0-4 Mountain West) · lost 21-10 to New Mexico · covered +14.5 · four straight losses · next: at Northern Illinois (0-6), Sun Oct 25, 4:30 a.m. JST (Sat Oct 24, 12:30 p.m. PT; 9:30 a.m. HT), MW+ · opened Hawai'i -6.5, total 45.5

What happened vs. New Mexico

  • A 10-0 halftime lead on homecoming night: Sean Olvera-Harle's 27-yd field goal and Micah Alejado's 1-yd TD pass to Titan Lacaden with 6 seconds left in the half. Hawai'i's defense held New Mexico scoreless for 30 minutes, with Jeremiah Hughes intercepting twice.
  • Then New Mexico ran: 243 rushing yards to Hawai'i's 48. Scottre Humphrey 15-90 with TDs of 1 and 49 yards; backup QB Toa Fa'avae (starting for the injured Jack Layne) ran for 77, including a 21-yd TD. New Mexico threw for only 94, exactly the backup-QB game plan from Lesson 27.
  • Alejado: 23 of 40, 250, TD, INT (on the final drive). Devin Alves 5-76, Kainoa Carvalho 3-49, Lacaden 7 catches. Jamih Otis and Wynden Ho'ohuli had 10 tackles each.
  • Betting: New Mexico -14.5 won by 11, so Hawai'i covered; 31 points went under 44.5–46.5. The brief passed both. Only 1 of Hawai'i's 7 games has gone over.
  • Timmy Chang: "Well fought, hard played game, that's a really good team on that other side, well coached." New Mexico is 5-1 (2-0).

Next: at Northern Illinois · Sun Oct 25, 4:30 a.m. JST (Sat Oct 24, 12:30 p.m. PT; 9:30 a.m. HT) · K5/MW+

  • Line: Hawai'i -6.5, total 45.5 (SportsBettingDime's opening table; the brief hasn't found a second book yet). It looks odd for a 1-6 road team until you see the other side: Northern Illinois is 0-6. Kickoff is 2:30 p.m. in DeKalb (Huskie Stadium), streaming on MW+.
  • Thursday check: no injury news found for either team since Monday; the brief still has only one book's number (Hawai'i -6.5, 45.5). Friday's edition has the lean or pass.
  • What to watch: Hawai'i has gone over the total once in 7 games and scores 16.7 a game; Micah Alejado has been sacked 17+ times. A lean (or a pass) comes Thursday or Friday once both teams' injury news is in.
New Mexico week notes (before the game)

Vs. New Mexico · Sun Oct 18, 11:30 a.m. JST (Sat Oct 17, 7:30 p.m. PT) · FS1

  • Game-day news (Tribune-Herald, Sat): New Mexico QB Jack Layne is out for the season (elbow); Toa Fa'avae starts. LB Jaxton Eck is listed out (shoulder), though the Albuquerque Journal had his coach optimistic earlier in the week. For Hawai'i, Micah Alejado starts (sacked 17 times this year), RB Cam Barfield is back, and the line is reshuffled (Boogie Henderson back in the rotation; Denaris DeRosa Jr. and Uluaki Taukiuvea new in the lineup). Line: New Mexico -14.5 (opened -16.5), total 44.5 at FanDuel and Caesars, 45.5 at DraftKings and BetMGM; moneyline UNM -650 to -770, Hawai'i +475 to +500. Brief: still a pass. The QB change already shows in the 2-point drop, and the brief can't find enough on Fa'avae to say the market missed it. Lesson 27 suggests New Mexico will lean on its run game, which is where Hawai'i's defense struggled at Arizona State (Cardae Mack 154 yards).
  • New Mexico is 4-1 under Jason Eck, the Mountain West's preseason media favorite, and got 3 AP votes this week. Now New Mexico -14.5 (opened -16.5), total 44.5 (opened 48.5); Dimers' models give New Mexico 86% and project about 30-15 (moneyline now UNM -675 / Hawai'i +550). The books have now posted moneylines: New Mexico -650 to -794, Hawai'i +475 to +521 (DraftKings +490, FanDuel +520), which works out to Hawai'i winning about 16–17% of the time and fits a spread of about 15–16. 97% of public bets are on New Mexico. Brief: no lean yet; the run defense that gave up 154 yards to Cardae Mack is the question.
  • The matchup inside the game: Micah Alejado throws a lot (44.2 attempts and 268.8 yards a game, 1,613 yards, 5 TDs, 59.2% completions), and New Mexico's defense leads the country in interception rate (9.6%) while allowing 154.8 passing yards a game. Alejado's props: 278.5–279 passing yards, 25 completions, 42.5 attempts (Doc's Sports leans Under attempts). That's the case for New Mexico covering: a high-volume passer against the best ball-hawking defense he's seen. Brief: pass on the spread; no prop edge either.
  • Saturday prices: New Mexico -14.5 to -15.5, total 44.5 (Under -115 at FanDuel), ML New Mexico -781 / Hawai'i +530. Kalshi: New Mexico 85¢, "New Mexico wins by over 14.5" 50¢, Over 44.5 52¢. numberFire gives New Mexico 91.9%. The numbers behind the low total: New Mexico scores 43.4 a game and allows 10.6 (2nd nationally); Hawai'i scores 16.7 (125th). Brief: pass; the spread is priced right, and Hawai'i has gone over only 1 of 6 times.
  • Hawai'i's problem is finishing: three straight losses and only 10 fourth-quarter TDs in six games (Star-Advertiser). Micah Alejado threw a 32-yard TD to Audric Harris at Arizona State; his 2-point pass was intercepted. No injuries reported.
  • Earlier: lost 20-16 to San José State (Alejado 338 yards, last-second Hail Mary batted down).

Weekly wrap: college Week 7 & NFL Week 6 (final)

College, Week 7 (final): the wildest Saturday in poll history

  • Five AP top-7 teams lost on the same day, a first since the poll began in 1936: No. 1 Georgia (27-24 to Auburn as a 19.5-point favorite), No. 2 Notre Dame (28-24 at BYU), No. 5 Indiana (19-15 to Ohio State), No. 6 Alabama (26-10 at Tennessee) and No. 7 Texas (27-24 to Florida).
  • How they lost: turnovers and run games again. Georgia 3 turnovers to Auburn's 0 and a Stockton fumble with 58 seconds left; Alabama 4 turnovers; Indiana 2 second-half fumbles; Texas a game-ending fumble; Notre Dame's CJ Carr threw 49 times without a TD. Tennessee's DeSean Bishop ran 32 times for 159 and Auburn's Byrum Brown 26 for 115.
  • Survived: Miami 43-35 over FSU in double overtime (Darian Mensah 326, 3 TDs; Malachi Toney 11-154), UCLA 17-7 with a backup QB, Houston 35-32 over Oklahoma State (Conner Weigman 5 TDs), Ole Miss 31-3 over Missouri, Oregon 17-14 over Nebraska on a TD with 28 seconds left.
  • Unbeaten now: Miami, BYU, Texas Tech, UCLA, Utah (all 6-0).
  • Betting week: underdogs getting points covered 10 of the 14 Saturday games the brief tracked; midweek and Friday underdogs covered 5 of 6. The brief went 1-0 on college (Penn State +6.5) and passed the rest.
  • Penn State and Hawai'i: Penn State lost 21-18 at Michigan (covered); Hawai'i lost 21-10 to New Mexico (covered). Details in their desks.

NFL, Week 6 (final)

  • Thursday: Broncos 17, Seahawks 14 in OT (brief's Broncos +3.5 won).
  • Sunday early: Texans 34-31 in London on Ka'imi Fairbairn's FG at the gun; Bears 31-23 over Atlanta (Caleb Williams 30 of 36); Browns 13-10 over Baltimore (Cleveland tied for the AFC North lead at 4-2); Titans 26-20 at Indianapolis, their first win; Patriots 26-19 over the Jets; Saints 34-27 at the Giants (4 takeaways); Panthers 25-14 at Philadelphia (the Eagles' 4th straight loss, first without Jalen Hurts); Steelers 26-19 in OT at Tampa (Jalen Ramsey 2 sacks).
  • The week's theme: points. 6 of 8 early games went over (one under, one right on the number), after weeks of low totals. Underdogs won 4 of the 8 outright.
  • Injuries today: Atlanta LB Kendal Daniels was taken off on a stretcher (team said he was alert with movement in all extremities); Philadelphia lost Nolan Smith (concussion) and Jordan Davis (ankle); Cleveland S Grant Delpit (concussion); Rams K Blake Grupe.
  • Sunday late and night: Cardinals 18-16 at the Rams as 12.5–13.5-point underdogs (Chad Ryland 6 FGs, the last at 0:00; the Rams lost K Blake Grupe and had their punter kick the go-ahead PAT); Chiefs 22-6 over the Chargers (Kenneth Walker III 128 yards, 2 TDs; LA 0-6); Bills 33-30 at Las Vegas (Josh Allen ties Jim Kelly with his 258th TD pass); Packers 23-13 over Dallas on Sunday night, 17.5 under the total.
  • Standings: division leaders Buffalo 5-1, Cleveland 4-2 (three-way tie with Baltimore and Pittsburgh), Jacksonville 4-2, Kansas City 5-0, Giants 3-2-1, Minnesota 5-0, Carolina 3-2, San Francisco 5-0. Winless: Chargers 0-6, Dolphins 0-5.
  • Injuries out of Week 6: Jalen Hurts on IR (blood clots; length disputed); Rams K Blake Grupe (knee); Dallas's KaVontae Turpin (rib) and LB Dee Winters (ankle); Arizona DT Walter Nolen III (ankle).
  • Monday night: 49ers 23, Commanders 17. San Francisco (6-0) led 20-3 at the half and hung on; Washington (+7.5) covered with two late Jayden Daniels TD passes (300 yards). The brief's Commanders +7.5 won. Mike Evans (Achilles) and Terry McLaurin (knee) got hurt.
  • Week 6 by the numbers: underdogs went 9-5 against the spread (Thursday's Broncos and Monday's Commanders plus 7-5 on Sunday), and six won outright (Denver, Houston, Cleveland, Tennessee, New Orleans, Arizona). Trends & metrics

  • Midweek college, all over the place: Tuesday's three games missed their totals by +3, +47 and -30.5, and two underdogs (MTSU +10, Marshall +2.5) won outright. The brief's rule for weeknight college games stays: a model gap alone is a pass.
  • Thursday totals: this season's two Thursday games the brief tracked went 8 and 9.5 under; tonight's total went up 1–3 points. That's why the Under is a pick at 50.5 and wasn't at 47.5.
  • The crowd's totals: Sunday night's total rose 2–3 points on Over money and finished 17.5 under; tonight's Monday-night total has risen from 50.5 to 51.5–52.5 with 75% of the money on the Over. A rising total isn't a reason to bet the Under by itself (Lesson 27), but it does mean the Over costs more than it did on Wednesday.
  • Big underdogs win more often than they feel like they should: Arizona (+12.5 to +13.5) won at the Rams a day after Auburn (+19.5) beat Georgia. A 13-point NFL underdog wins outright roughly 1 time in 7 or 8; Sunday was one of those.
  • Kickers decided Week 6: Chad Ryland's 6 FGs, the Rams playing without their kicker, Ka'imi Fairbairn's game-winner in London and Cam Little's 60-yarder. A team's kicker situation belongs on the injury check for close spreads and totals.

Both had weak run defenses across from them. The rule (weak run defense and a team likely to lead) now gets a hard stop: if the starting QB news makes the other team better, the prop is a pass.

  • Game script beats matchup for running-back props: the brief picked Kyren Williams 70+ because Buffalo's run defense ranked 28th–30th. But the Rams trailed and threw 54 times, so he got 16 carries for 49. Bijan Robinson's 70+ won Sunday because Atlanta led. Rule going forward: a running-back over needs both a weak run defense and a team likely to lead; one isn't enough.

But the three outright underdog winners (Packers, 49ers, Ravens) were the games where the dog had the better defense or the market overreacted to a QB change. The brief's rule still holds: take points in close, strength-on-strength games, not just any underdog.

Totals that dropped 4–7 points on forecasts overshot. Market 101, Lesson 12's rule (sustained wind over 15 mph, measured at kickoff) matters more than the forecast.
  • Turnovers drove totals over: 49ers-Seahawks had 7 turnovers and finished 14.5 over; short fields are points (Lesson 9).
  • The brief's week: 7-2, +1.39 units. Six of the seven wins were underdogs getting points; five of those won outright (Bucs, UCLA, USC, Georgia, 49ers).
